      - **Bottom-up** (IFRS 17.B80): liquid risk-free yield curve **plus** an illiquidity adjustment for the difference between the liquidity of the instruments underlying the observed rates and that of the insurance contracts.
      - **Top-down** (IFRS 17.B81): yield curve implicit in the fair value of a **reference portfolio** of assets, adjusted to strip out factors not relevant to the insurance contracts (notably credit risk (IFRS 17.B83(b))). Critically, under the top-down approach the entity is **not required** to adjust for differences in liquidity between the reference portfolio and the contracts.
      - The two approaches may produce **different** curves in the same currency, and the entity is **not required to reconcile** them (IFRS 17.B84). The reference portfolio is unrestricted, but debt instruments require fewer adjustments than equities (IFRS 17.B85).
    - **Risk adjustment for non-financial risk (RA)** (IFRS 17.37, ¶B86–B92): the compensation the entity requires for bearing uncertainty about the amount and timing of cash flows from **non-financial** risk — insurance risk, lapse risk, expense risk (IFRS 17.B86). Defined as the amount that makes the entity indifferent between fulfilling a liability with a range of outcomes and one with fixed cash flows of the same expected present value (IFRS 17.B87). It reflects the entity's own degree of diversification benefit and risk aversion (IFRS 17.B88), must **exclude** general operational risk (IFRS 17.B89), must be **explicit** and must not be double-counted inside the cash flow estimates or the discount rate (IFRS 17.B90). No technique is mandated, but the result must satisfy: low-frequency/high-severity risks give a higher RA than high-frequency/low-severity; longer duration gives a higher RA than shorter for similar risks; and wider probability distributions give a higher RA than narrower (IFRS 17.B91).
  - **Contractual service margin (CSM)** (IFRS 17.38): on initial recognition, the amount that results in **no income or expense** arising from the initial recognition of the FCF, the cash flows arising at that date, and the derecognition of any asset for insurance acquisition cash flows or other pre-recognition asset/liability. If that calculation would give a negative CSM, the group is onerous: CSM is nil and the loss goes straight to P&L (IFRS 17.47).
  - **Subsequent carrying amount** (IFRS 17.40) = **liability for remaining coverage (LRC)** — FCF related to future service plus the CSM — **plus** the **liability for incurred claims (LIC)** — FCF related to past service.
  - **Where movements go** (IFRS 17.41–42): changes in the LRC produce insurance revenue (reduction because services were provided), insurance service expenses (losses on onerous groups and reversals) and insurance finance income or expenses (time value of money and financial risk). Changes in the LIC produce insurance service expenses (claims and expenses incurred, excluding investment components; and subsequent changes in FCF relating to incurred claims) and insurance finance income or expenses.

- **CSM roll-forward — what adjusts it and what does not** (this is the mechanic auditors test hardest):
  - **GMM, contracts without direct participation features** (IFRS 17.44) — opening CSM, then in order: (a) effect of new contracts added to the group; (b) **interest accreted at the locked-in rate** — the discount rates determined at the date of initial recognition of the group, applied to nominal cash flows that do not vary with returns on underlying items (IFRS 17.B72(b)); (c) changes in FCF **relating to future service** (IFRS 17.B96), measured at those same **locked-in** initial-recognition rates (IFRS 17.B72(c)); (d) currency exchange differences on the CSM; and (e) the amount released to insurance revenue for services provided, allocated over coverage units (IFRS 17.B119). Note the sequencing: the release in (e) is calculated on the CSM **after** all other adjustments.
  - **Changes that DO adjust the CSM** (IFRS 17.B96): experience adjustments on **premiums received in the period that relate to future service** (and related acquisition cash flows and premium-based taxes); changes in estimates of the present value of future cash flows in the LRC; differences between the investment component expected to become payable in the period and the actual amount that becomes payable; the equivalent difference for policyholder loans; and changes in the RA relating to future service. Discretionary cash flows: a change in the cash flows an entity has discretion over is treated as relating to **future** service and adjusts the CSM — which requires the entity to have specified at inception the basis on which it determines its commitment (e.g. a fixed rate, or specified asset returns) (IFRS 17.B98).
  - **Changes that do NOT adjust the CSM** (IFRS 17.B97) — these go to P&L or OCI: the effect of time value of money and financial risk, including the effect on estimated cash flows, on the RA (if disaggregated) and of a change in discount rate; changes in estimates of FCF in the **liability for incurred claims**; and all experience adjustments other than the future-service premium adjustments in ¶B96(a).
  - **Floor at zero and the loss component** (IFRS 17.48–52): the CSM cannot go negative. Unfavourable future-service changes (and, for VFA, a decrease in the entity's share of the fair value of underlying items) in excess of the carrying CSM are recognised as a **loss in P&L** and establish or increase a **loss component** of the LRC (IFRS 17.49). Thereafter: (i) subsequent changes in the FCF of the LRC — namely the release of the PV of future claims/expenses because of incurred insurance service expenses, the RA release from risk, and insurance finance income or expenses — are allocated on a **systematic basis** between the loss component and the remainder of the LRC (IFRS 17.50(a), ¶51); and (ii) subsequent **favourable** future-service changes, and subsequent increases in the entity's share of the fair value of underlying items, are allocated **solely to the loss component until it is nil**, with only the excess reinstating the CSM (IFRS 17.50(b)). The systematic allocation must be calibrated so that amounts allocated to the loss component total **zero by the end of the coverage period** (IFRS 17.52). Amounts in the loss component are excluded from insurance revenue (IFRS 17.49, ¶B123(b)).
  - **Coverage units and the release pattern** (IFRS 17.B119): identify the coverage units in the group — the **quantity of insurance contract services** provided, determined by considering, for each contract, the *quantity of benefits* provided and its *expected coverage period*; allocate the end-of-period CSM (before any release) **equally to each coverage unit** provided in the current period and expected in the future; recognise the amount allocated to the current period in P&L. Coverage units are not premiums, not claims and not a straight-line time allocation unless the benefit pattern happens to be level.
    - **Investment-return service** extends the coverage period beyond insurance coverage for contracts **without** direct participation features, but only if all three of: an investment component exists or the policyholder has a right to withdraw an amount; the entity expects that amount to include an investment return (which may be negative); and the entity expects to **perform investment activity** to generate it (IFRS 17.B119B). The service period ends at or before the date all amounts due to **current** policyholders for those services have been paid (IFRS 17.B119A).
    - Where the quantity of benefits per contract is the same across a group, the coverage units for a period reduce to the **number of contracts** for which coverage is expected to be provided in that period (Illustrative Example IE58).
    - **A level release pattern is permissible.** Allocating the CSM strictly per ¶B119 produces an *increasing* profile, because interest accretes before each period's release. The illustrative examples state that an entity may instead allocate equally in each period the CSM **including the total interest expected to be accreted over the coverage period**, and still achieve the objective — in the worked example this converts an 82 / 86 / 91 profile into a level 86 in each of three years. Useful where a level release better matches the service pattern; it is an allocation-method choice, not a departure from ¶B119.
    - IFRIC agenda decision, June 2022 (*Transfer of Insurance Coverage under a Group of Annuity Contracts*): for a group of annuities where the policyholder receives only survival-contingent periodic payments, the coverage units reflect the ability to receive those payments; for a **deferred** annuity the coverage period is not limited to the payout phase. Reproduced inline in the annotated standard at ¶B119.

- **Premium Allocation Approach (PAA) — simplified LRC measurement** (IFRS 17.53–59):
  - **Eligibility — two independent routes** (IFRS 17.53). The group qualifies if, **at the inception of the group**, *either*:
    - **(a) the materiality route:** the entity reasonably expects the simplification would produce a measurement of the **liability for remaining coverage** that would **not differ materially** from GMM; or
    - **(b) the bright line:** the **coverage period of each contract** in the group — including services arising from all premiums within the contract boundary determined at that date under ¶34 — is **one year or less**.
    - The test is on the **LRC only**, not the whole liability; the LIC is measured on essentially GMM principles either way (IFRS 17.59(b)).
  - **What disqualifies a group** (IFRS 17.54): the ¶53(a) materiality route **is not met** if, at inception, the entity expects **significant variability in the fulfilment cash flows** that would affect the measurement of the LRC **during the period before a claim is incurred**. The standard names two drivers that increase that variability: the extent of future cash flows relating to any **embedded derivatives**, and the **length of the coverage period**. Route (b) is unaffected — a group of contracts each of one year or less is eligible regardless of variability. Note that ¶54 disqualifies only route (a); a failed route (a) does not disqualify a group that passes route (b), and vice versa.
  - **LRC measurement** (IFRS 17.55): initially premiums received, less insurance acquisition cash flows (unless expensed under ¶59(a)), plus or minus amounts arising from derecognition of any asset for insurance acquisition cash flows or other pre-recognition asset/liability. Subsequently: plus premiums received; minus acquisition cash flows; plus amortisation of acquisition cash flows recognised as expense; plus any financing-component adjustment; **minus insurance revenue for services provided** (IFRS 17.B126); minus any investment component paid or transferred to the LIC.
  - **Discounting the LRC** (IFRS 17.56): required only if the contracts have a **significant financing component**, using the discount rates determined on initial recognition (IFRS 17.B72(d)). Practical expedient: no adjustment is required if, at initial recognition, the entity expects the time between providing each part of the services and the related premium due date to be **no more than a year**.
  - **Acquisition cash flow expedient** (IFRS 17.59(a)): may be expensed as incurred, but **only** if the coverage period of each contract in the group at initial recognition is no more than one year.
  - **LIC under PAA** (IFRS 17.59(b)): measured at fulfilment cash flows — so a **risk adjustment is still required**. Discounting is not required if the cash flows are expected to be paid or received **within one year of the date the claims are incurred**.
  - **Insurance revenue under PAA** (IFRS 17.B126–B127): the expected premium receipts — excluding any investment component, and adjusted for the time value of money and financial risk where ¶56 applies — allocated to each period of insurance contract services **on the basis of the passage of time**; but where the expected pattern of release of risk over the coverage period differs **significantly** from the passage of time, on the basis of the **expected timing of incurred insurance service expenses**. The basis must be changed as facts and circumstances change (IFRS 17.B127). Insurance revenue related to insurance acquisition cash flows is a separate allocation of the premium portion recovering them, on a systematic passage-of-time basis, with an equal amount recognised in insurance service expenses (IFRS 17.B125).
  - **Onerous test under PAA** (IFRS 17.57–58): if at any time during the coverage period facts and circumstances indicate a group is onerous, compare the ¶55 LRC carrying amount with the FCF relating to remaining coverage; the excess of the FCF over the carrying amount is a **loss in P&L** and increases the LRC. (If the entity is not discounting the LIC under ¶59(b), it must not include a time-value adjustment in those FCF.)

- **Variable Fee Approach (VFA) — insurance contracts with direct participation features** (IFRS 17.B101–B118):
  - **Eligibility — all three conditions** (IFRS 17.B101). These are contracts that are substantially investment-related service contracts under which the entity promises an investment return based on underlying items:
    - (a) the contractual terms specify that the policyholder participates in a **share of a clearly identified pool of underlying items**;
    - (b) the entity expects to pay the policyholder an amount equal to a **substantial share of the fair value returns** on the underlying items; and
    - (c) the entity expects a **substantial proportion of any change** in the amounts payable to the policyholder **to vary with the change in fair value** of the underlying items.
  - **Assessed at inception using expectations then, and never reassessed** unless the contract is modified under ¶72 (IFRS 17.B102). "Substantial" in (b) and (c) is judged against the objective of the model — that the entity provides investment-related services compensated by a fee determined by reference to the underlying items — and assessed **over the duration of the contract** and on a **present-value probability-weighted average basis**, not on a best- or worst-case outcome (IFRS 17.B107).
  - **Clearly identified pool** (IFRS 17.B105–B106): may be a reference portfolio, the entity's net assets, or a specified subset of them; the entity **need not hold** the pool, but the link must be **enforceable**. A clearly identified pool does **not** exist where the entity can change the underlying items retrospectively, or where no underlying items are identified even though the policyholder receives a return reflecting the entity's overall performance — e.g. a crediting rate or dividend set at the end of the period to which it relates (IFRS 17.B106(b)). This is the most common VFA failure.
  - **Reinsurance contracts issued and reinsurance contracts held can never be VFA** (IFRS 17.B109). A cedant applies GMM to reinsurance held even where the underlying contracts are VFA.
  - **How the CSM absorbs changes in the underlying items** — the obligation is decomposed (IFRS 17.B104) into (a) the obligation to pay the policyholder an amount equal to the **fair value of the underlying items**, and (b) a **variable fee** the entity deducts from (a) for future service, comprising the entity's share of the fair value of the underlying items less fulfilment cash flows that do not vary with returns on underlying items. From that decomposition:
    - Changes in (a) — the policyholder's share of the fair value of underlying items — **do not** relate to future service and **do not** adjust the CSM (IFRS 17.B111). They flow through insurance finance income or expenses.
    - Changes in the **entity's share** of the fair value of underlying items **do** relate to future service and **do** adjust the CSM (IFRS 17.B112, ¶45(b)).
    - Changes in FCF that do **not** vary with returns on underlying items adjust the CSM to the extent they relate to future service under the same ¶B96–B97 logic as GMM, **but measured at current discount rates**; and the change in the effect of time value of money and financial risk **not** arising from the underlying items (e.g. the cost of **financial guarantees**) also relates to future service and adjusts the CSM (IFRS 17.B113).
    - Net effect: under VFA the CSM absorbs both the entity's share of investment performance and the movement in financial guarantee costs, so market movements are largely deferred rather than hitting P&L. This is the "natural hedge" — but only for the entity's share.
  - **VFA CSM roll-forward** (IFRS 17.45): (a) new contracts; (b) change in the entity's share of the fair value of underlying items; (c) future-service changes in FCF per ¶B101–B118; (d) currency differences; (e) the release for services provided per ¶B119 — each of (b) and (c) capped so the CSM cannot go negative, with the excess a loss under ¶48 and subsequent reversals under ¶50(b). **There is no separate interest-accretion line and no locked-in rate under VFA** — the CSM is remeasured through the underlying-items adjustment instead. The entity is not required to identify these adjustments separately and may compute a combined amount (IFRS 17.45, ¶B114).
  - **Risk mitigation option** (IFRS 17.B115–B118): where the entity mitigates the effect of financial risk using derivatives, non-derivative financial instruments at FVTPL, or reinsurance contracts held, it may elect **not** to adjust the CSM for some or all of the resulting changes in the effect of time value of money and financial risk — on the entity's share of underlying items (derivatives or reinsurance held only) and on the ¶B113(b) fulfilment cash flows (all three instrument types). Conditions (IFRS 17.B116): a **previously documented** risk-management objective and strategy; an **economic offset** between the insurance contracts and the mitigating item (assessed ignoring accounting measurement differences); and credit risk must **not dominate** that offset. The determination must be applied consistently each period (IFRS 17.B117), and the option must cease prospectively the moment any ¶B116 condition fails, with **no** retrospective adjustment (IFRS 17.B118). Presentation: insurance finance income or expenses arising from ¶B115 goes to **P&L** where the hedging instrument is a derivative or non-derivative at FVTPL; where reinsurance held is the mitigating item, it follows the same OCI policy as that reinsurance (IFRS 17.B117A, ¶87A(a)).

- **Reinsurance contracts held** (IFRS 17.60–70A) — measured separately from the underlying contracts, and never offset against them:
  - **Grouping:** portfolios are divided under ¶14–24 but with "onerous" replaced by "**a net gain on initial recognition**"; a group may be a single contract (IFRS 17.61).
  - **Recognition** (IFRS 17.62): from the **earlier** of the beginning of the coverage period, and the date the entity recognises an **onerous group of underlying contracts** — provided the reinsurance was entered into at or before that date. For **proportionate** coverage, recognition is delayed until any underlying contract is initially recognised, if that is later (IFRS 17.62A).
  - **Measurement modifications:** use **consistent assumptions** with the underlying contracts, and include the effect of the reinsurer's **risk of non-performance**, including collateral and losses from disputes (IFRS 17.63). The RA represents the amount of risk **transferred** to the reinsurer, not the amount retained (IFRS 17.64).
  - **CSM = net cost or net gain of purchasing reinsurance**, not unearned profit (IFRS 17.65). A **net gain** is therefore deferred as a CSM, not recognised immediately. Exception: where the net cost relates to events that occurred **before** the purchase, it is expensed immediately (IFRS 17.65A).
  - **Reinsurance held can never be onerous** — ¶47–52 do not apply (IFRS 17.68). Changes from the reinsurer's non-performance risk do **not** relate to future service and do **not** adjust the CSM; they go to P&L (IFRS 17.67).
  - **Loss-recovery component** (IFRS 17.66A–66B, ¶B119C–B119F): when the entity recognises a loss on initial recognition of an onerous group of underlying contracts (or on adding onerous contracts to a group), it adjusts the reinsurance CSM and recognises **income** immediately. **Critical eligibility condition:** ¶66A applies **if and only if the reinsurance contract held was entered into before or at the same time as** the onerous underlying contracts are recognised (IFRS 17.B119C). Retrospectively purchased reinsurance gets no loss recovery. The amount is the loss on the underlying contracts × the percentage of claims on those contracts the entity expects to recover from the reinsurance group (IFRS 17.B119D); where an onerous group mixes reinsured and non-reinsured contracts, a systematic and rational allocation is required (IFRS 17.B119E). The resulting **loss-recovery component of the asset for remaining coverage** (IFRS 17.66B) is adjusted for changes in the underlying loss component and is **capped** at the portion of that loss component the entity expects to recover (IFRS 17.B119F). Under PAA for reinsurance held, ¶66A is applied by adjusting the **asset for remaining coverage** instead of the CSM (IFRS 17.70A).
  - **PAA for reinsurance held** (IFRS 17.69–70): same two routes as ¶53, adapted — the "not materially different" test is against ¶63–68, and the one-year bright line applies to the reinsurance coverage period. The ¶70 disqualifier mirrors ¶54: significant expected variability in FCF affecting the **asset** for remaining coverage before a claim is incurred.

- **Presentation — statement of financial position** (IFRS 17.78): present separately the carrying amount of **portfolios** (not groups) of: insurance contracts issued that are **assets**; insurance contracts issued that are **liabilities**; reinsurance contracts held that are **assets**; and reinsurance contracts held that are **liabilities**. Assets for insurance acquisition cash flows are included within the carrying amount of the related **portfolios** of insurance contracts issued (IFRS 17.79).

- **Presentation — statement(s) of financial performance** (IFRS 17.80–92):
  - Disaggregate into an **insurance service result** (insurance revenue less insurance service expenses) and **insurance finance income or expenses** (IFRS 17.80). Disaggregating the change in the RA between the two is **optional**; if not disaggregated, the whole change sits in the insurance service result (IFRS 17.81).
  - Income or expenses from **reinsurance held** are presented separately from those on contracts issued (IFRS 17.82). They may be shown as a **single net amount**, or split between amounts recovered from the reinsurer and an allocation of premiums paid — in which case reinsurance cash flows contingent on underlying claims are treated as part of expected recoveries, non-contingent amounts (e.g. some ceding commissions) reduce the premiums paid, loss-recovery amounts are treated as amounts recovered, and the **allocation of premiums paid must not be presented as a reduction in revenue** (IFRS 17.86).
  - **Insurance revenue** (IFRS 17.83, ¶B120–B126): total revenue for a group is the consideration — premiums adjusted for a financing effect and **excluding investment components** (IFRS 17.B120). It covers (a) amounts related to services — insurance service expenses, income tax specifically chargeable to the policyholder, the RA release, and the CSM released, each excluding amounts allocated to the loss component — and (b) amounts related to insurance acquisition cash flows (IFRS 17.B121). Equivalently, revenue is the reduction in the LRC in the period **excluding** changes that do not relate to services provided (premiums received, investment components, policyholder loan cash flows, transaction-based taxes collected for third parties, insurance finance income/expenses, acquisition cash flows, derecognition of liabilities transferred) and **excluding** changes for which no consideration is expected — that is, movements in the **loss component** (IFRS 17.B123). Premium information must not be presented in P&L if it is inconsistent with ¶83 (IFRS 17.85).
  - **Insurance service expenses** (IFRS 17.84): incurred claims excluding repayments of investment components, other incurred insurance service expenses, and the ¶103(b) items (amortisation of acquisition cash flows, changes relating to past service, and losses/reversals on onerous groups).
  - **Insurance finance income or expenses** (IFRS 17.87): the change in carrying amount from the time value of money and its changes, and from financial risk and its changes — **excluding**, for VFA groups, changes that would have adjusted the CSM but did not because the CSM hit its floor or was reinstated (¶45(b)(ii)/(iii), 45(c)(ii)/(iii)); those go to **insurance service expenses**.
  - **The OCI option** (IFRS 17.87A–92): an accounting policy choice, applied **per portfolio** (IFRS 17.B129), between (a) recognising all insurance finance income or expenses in P&L, or (b) disaggregating — putting in P&L an amount determined by a **systematic allocation** of the expected total insurance finance income or expenses over the duration of the group (IFRS 17.88(b), ¶B130–B133), with the difference in OCI (IFRS 17.90). For **direct participating contracts where the entity holds the underlying items**, the (b) alternative is instead an amount that **eliminates accounting mismatches** with the P&L income or expenses on the underlying items held (IFRS 17.89(b), ¶B134–B136). Amounts arising from the ¶B115 risk mitigation option are outside this choice and follow ¶B117A (IFRS 17.87A(a)).
    - **Recycling asymmetry** (IFRS 17.91): on transfer or derecognition of a group, amounts previously in OCI under the ¶88(b) policy **are** reclassified to P&L; amounts under the ¶89(b) policy **are not**.
    - Exchange differences on insurance contract groups go to P&L, except to the extent they relate to changes recognised in OCI under ¶90 (IFRS 17.92). Note ¶30: an insurance contract group, **including the CSM**, is a **monetary item** under IAS 21 — see the September 2022 IFRIC agenda decision on multi-currency groups, which requires an accounting policy determining the currency denomination of the group and prohibits simply presuming the CSM is denominated in the functional currency.

- **IFRS 18 interaction (effective 1 January 2027)** — relevant to every insurer's 2027 primary statements:
  - IFRS 18 requires **five separate line items** for IFRS 17 amounts in the statement of profit or loss: insurance revenue; insurance service expenses from contracts issued; income or expenses from reinsurance contracts held; insurance finance income or expenses from contracts issued; and finance income or expenses from reinsurance contracts held (IFRS 18.75(c)).
  - **Insurance finance income and expenses recognised in P&L under IFRS 17 are excluded from the financing category and classified in the operating category** (IFRS 18.64(b)) — alongside income and expenses from issued investment contracts with participation features accounted for under IFRS 9.
  - **Insurance service result** and **net financial result** (investment income less insurance finance income and expenses) are named as subtotals "similar to gross profit", so they are **not** management-defined performance measures and do not attract the MPM disclosures (IFRS 18.B123(c)–(d)).
  - IFRS 18 amended IFRS 17 ¶91, 96, 103 and B129, applied when IFRS 18 is applied (IFRS 17.C2B). In the post-amendment text, **¶96** points to **IFRS 18.41–43** for aggregation and disaggregation of information (replacing IAS 1.29–31, and dropping the former "materiality" wording), and **¶B129** points to **IAS 8** under its new name, *Basis of Preparation of Financial Statements*, for the ¶13 consistency requirement. **¶91** replaces the IAS 1 reclassification-adjustment reference with IFRS 18.
  - Insurers should separately assess the IFRS 18 "**specified main business activities**" tests (investing in assets, providing financing to customers), which drive classification of investment income and interest expense. That assessment is outside IFRS 17 and was the subject of a March 2026 IFRIC agenda decision on IFRS 18.

- **Transition** [Appendix C]:
  - **Date of initial application** = the beginning of the annual period in which IFRS 17 is first applied; **transition date** = the beginning of the immediately preceding annual period (IFRS 17.C2).
  - **Full retrospective approach is the default and is mandatory unless impracticable** (or unless ¶C5A applies) (IFRS 17.C3). Apply IFRS 17 as if it had always applied: identify, recognise and measure each group — and each asset for insurance acquisition cash flows — retrospectively; derecognise balances that would not exist; put the net difference in **equity** (IFRS 17.C4). Two carve-outs even under full retrospective application: the IAS 8.28(f) (or IFRS 19.178(f)) quantitative impact information is not required, and the ¶B115 risk mitigation option **cannot** be applied to periods before the transition date — only prospectively, and only where risk mitigation relationships are designated at or before the date the option is first applied (IFRS 17.C3(b)).
  - **Modified retrospective approach (MRA)** (IFRS 17.C6–C19A), available **only where full retrospective application is impracticable for that group** (IFRS 17.C5(a)). Objective: the closest possible outcome to retrospective application using reasonable and supportable information available without undue cost or effort, maximising use of the information that full retrospective application would have used (IFRS 17.C6). **Hard constraint:** each specified modification may be used **only to the extent the entity does not have reasonable and supportable information to apply the retrospective approach** (IFRS 17.C8) — the modifications are not a menu. And **if the entity cannot obtain the reasonable and supportable information the MRA needs, it must use the fair value approach instead** (IFRS 17.C6(a)). Key permitted modifications:
    - Determine grouping, VFA classification, discretionary cash flow identification and investment-contract-with-DPF classification using information available **at the transition date** (IFRS 17.C9).
    - **Do not apply ¶22** — groups may include contracts issued more than one year apart (IFRS 17.C10). This is the transition annual-cohorts relief.
    - Estimate initial-recognition cash flows by rolling the transition-date cash flows **backwards** for cash flows known to have occurred, including from contracts that ceased to exist before transition (IFRS 17.C12).
    - Determine initial-recognition **discount rates** using an observable yield curve that approximated the IFRS 17 curve for **at least three years** immediately before the transition date; failing that, using an average spread between an observable curve and the IFRS 17 curve, averaged over **at least three years** (IFRS 17.C13).
    - Determine the initial-recognition **RA** by adjusting the transition-date RA for the expected release of risk before transition, referenced to similar contracts issued at the transition date (IFRS 17.C14).
  - **Fair value approach (FVA)** (IFRS 17.C20–C24B): the CSM (or loss component) at the transition date is the **difference between the fair value of the group and its fulfilment cash flows at that date**, determined **without** applying IFRS 13.47 (the demand-feature floor) (IFRS 17.C20). Available where full retrospective is impracticable (IFRS 17.C5(b)), and — notably — **also available by free choice, even where retrospective application is practicable**, for a group of **direct participating contracts** where the entity elects the ¶B115 risk mitigation option prospectively from the transition date and had actually used derivatives, non-derivative FVTPL instruments or reinsurance held to mitigate that financial risk before the transition date (IFRS 17.C5A).
    - FVA reliefs: grouping and classification determinations may be made using inception-date information **or** transition-date information, at the entity's choice (IFRS 17.C21–C22); ¶22 need not be applied, so groups may span more than one year of issue, and the entity divides into annual cohorts only if it has reasonable and supportable information to do so (IFRS 17.C23); discount rates for ¶B72(b)–B72(e)(ii) and the incurred-claim rates in ¶B72(e)(iii) may be determined at the **transition date** rather than at initial recognition (IFRS 17.C23); and the cumulative OCI amount may be determined retrospectively (only with reasonable and supportable information), as **nil**, or — for VFA contracts under ¶B134 — as equal to the cumulative OCI on the underlying items (IFRS 17.C24). A loss-recovery component is established at transition **without** needing to satisfy the ¶B119C timing condition (IFRS 17.C20A).
  - **Consequences for the CSM — the point that drives reported profit for a decade:** full retrospective application generally produces the **largest** transition CSM (and hence the largest deferred future profit) because it rebuilds the historical margin; the MRA approximates it; the FVA typically produces the **smallest** CSM, because a market participant's required margin is normally lower than the accumulated historical margin, and because the FVA reliefs (transition-date discount rates, nil cumulative OCI, wider groups) compress it further. The FVA reliefs on grouping also permanently embed non-annual cohorts into the run-off, which changes the release pattern for years. Because of this, IFRS 17 requires the CSM reconciliation (IFRS 17.101(c)) and insurance revenue (IFRS 17.103(a)) to be disclosed **separately** for MRA groups, FVA groups and all other contracts, for as long as those groups exist (IFRS 17.114), with an explanation of how the transition measurement was determined (IFRS 17.115).
  - **Financial asset redesignation** (IFRS 17.C29): at the date of initial application of IFRS 17, an entity that had already applied IFRS 9 may reassess the business model condition for **eligible** financial assets (those not held in respect of an activity unconnected with IFRS 17 contracts — so **not** banking assets or assets in funds backing out-of-scope investment contracts); must revoke a previous FVTPL designation where the accounting-mismatch condition no longer holds; and may make new FVTPL or FVOCI-equity designations.
  - **Classification overlay** (IFRS 17.C28A–C28E): an entity first applying IFRS 17 and IFRS 9 at the same time may present comparative information for a financial asset **as if** IFRS 9 classification and measurement had applied, where that asset's comparatives were not restated (because prior periods were not restated, or because the asset was derecognised in the comparative period). It uses reasonable and supportable information available at the transition date; applying the IFRS 9 **impairment** requirements is optional, with any IAS 39 impairment carried forward if they are not applied (IFRS 17.C28C); the difference goes to opening retained earnings (IFRS 17.C28D); and it may be applied only to comparative periods between the IFRS 17 transition date and the date of initial application (IFRS 17.C28E(b)).

- **Interim reporting** (IFRS 17.B137): an accounting policy choice — applied to **all** groups issued and all reinsurance held — as to whether to change the treatment of accounting estimates made in previous interim financial statements when applying IFRS 17 in later interims and in the annual period. Choosing not to change them ("frozen" interims) means the annual result is not simply the sum of a fresh annual calculation.

**Disclosure requirements:**
- **Reconciliations** (IFRS 17.98–105B) — separately for insurance contracts issued and reinsurance contracts held, presented **in a table**, with opening and closing net carrying amounts split between portfolios that are assets and portfolios that are liabilities, tying to the ¶78 statement of financial position (IFRS 17.99):
  - **Always required** (IFRS 17.100): (a) net LRC excluding any loss component; (b) the **loss component**; (c) the liability for incurred claims — and for PAA groups, (c) is split further into the estimates of the present value of future cash flows and the risk adjustment.
  - **Non-PAA only** (IFRS 17.101): additional reconciliations of (a) estimates of the present value of future cash flows; (b) the risk adjustment; and (c) the **contractual service margin**.
  - **Analysis of movements** (IFRS 17.103) within the ¶100 reconciliation: insurance revenue; and insurance service expenses split into incurred claims (excluding investment components) and other incurred expenses, amortisation of acquisition cash flows, changes relating to **past** service (changes in FCF for the LIC), and changes relating to **future** service (losses on onerous groups and reversals).
  - **Non-PAA analysis** (IFRS 17.104) within the ¶101 reconciliation: changes relating to future service, split between changes in estimates that **adjust** the CSM, changes that **do not** adjust it (onerous-group losses and reversals), and the effects of **contracts initially recognised in the period**; and changes relating to current service.
  - **Non-service items** (IFRS 17.105): cash flows (premiums received/paid, acquisition cash flows, claims and expenses paid/recovered); the effect of changes in the reinsurer's non-performance risk; and insurance finance income or expenses.
  - **Asset for insurance acquisition cash flows** (IFRS 17.105A–105B): opening-to-closing reconciliation, with impairment losses and reversals shown separately.
- **Insurance revenue analysis** (non-PAA, contracts issued) (IFRS 17.106): the ¶B124 components — insurance service expenses incurred in the period measured at the amounts expected at the beginning of the period; the change in the RA; the CSM recognised in P&L for services transferred; other amounts — plus the allocation of the premium portion recovering **insurance acquisition cash flows** (IFRS 17.B125).
- **Effect of contracts initially recognised in the period** (non-PAA) (IFRS 17.107): separately for contracts issued and reinsurance held, the effect at initial recognition on the estimates of the PV of future cash **outflows** (with acquisition cash flows shown separately), the PV of future cash **inflows**, the RA, and the CSM.
- **CSM run-off** (IFRS 17.109): **quantitatively, in appropriate time bands**, when the entity expects to recognise the closing CSM in P&L — separately for contracts issued and reinsurance held. Time bands are also required for expected derecognition of the asset for insurance acquisition cash flows (IFRS 17.109A).
- **Insurance finance income or expenses** (IFRS 17.110): the total amount, with an explanation of its **relationship to the investment return on the entity's assets**. If the OCI option is used, an explanation of the methods used to determine the P&L amount (IFRS 17.118).
- **Direct participating contracts** (IFRS 17.111–113): composition and **fair value of the underlying items**; where the ¶B115 risk mitigation option is used, the **effect of that choice on the CSM adjustment** in the period (IFRS 17.112); and, on a change in the basis of disaggregating insurance finance income or expenses, the reason the change was required, the amount of any adjustment for each financial statement line item affected, and the carrying amount of the group of insurance contracts to which the change applied at the date of the change (IFRS 17.113).
- **Transition** (IFRS 17.114–116): the ¶101(c) CSM reconciliation and ¶103(a) insurance revenue disclosed **separately** for (a) contracts at transition measured under the **modified retrospective** approach, (b) those measured under the **fair value** approach, and (c) all other contracts; plus an explanation of how the transition-date measurement was determined, for all periods in which those disclosures are made (IFRS 17.115).
- **PAA-specific** (IFRS 17.97): only ¶98–100, 102–103, 105–105B and 109A apply to PAA contracts. In addition, a PAA user must disclose **which of the ¶53 / ¶69 criteria it satisfied** (i.e. the materiality route or the one-year route); whether it adjusts for the time value of money and financial risk under ¶56, 57(b) and 59(b); and the method chosen for recognising insurance acquisition cash flows under ¶59(a).
- **Significant judgements** (IFRS 17.117–120): the methods used to measure contracts and the processes for estimating inputs, with **quantitative information about those inputs** unless impracticable; changes in methods and processes, the reason for each and the contract types affected (IFRS 17.117); the **confidence level** used to determine the risk adjustment — and where a technique other than a confidence level technique is used, the technique **and the confidence level corresponding to its result** (IFRS 17.119); and the **yield curve (or range of curves)** used to discount cash flows that do not vary with returns on underlying items, in weighted averages or relatively narrow ranges where given in aggregate (IFRS 17.120).
- **Nature and extent of risks** (IFRS 17.121–132): exposures and how they arise, objectives/policies/processes for managing them, methods used to measure them, and changes in any of these (IFRS 17.124); **summary quantitative information based on what is reported internally to key management personnel** (IFRS 17.125(a)); a statement where the period-end exposure is not representative of exposure during the period, with the reason and representative information (IFRS 17.123); **concentrations of risk**, including how concentrations are determined and the shared characteristic identifying each (IFRS 17.127); a **sensitivity analysis** showing the effect on profit or loss and equity of reasonably possible changes in risk variables — for insurance risk, before and after mitigation by reinsurance held; for each type of market risk, in a way that explains the relationship to sensitivities arising from the entity's financial assets (IFRS 17.128) (an internally used alternative analysis may be substituted, with an explanation of its method, parameters, objective and limitations (IFRS 17.129)); a **claims development** disclosure comparing actual claims with previous estimates of the undiscounted amounts, starting from the earliest period in which a material claim arose that is still uncertain but **not required to go back more than 10 years**, not required for claims typically resolved within one year, and reconciled to the ¶100(c) LIC carrying amount (IFRS 17.130); **credit risk** — maximum exposure, separately for contracts issued and reinsurance held (IFRS 17.131); and **liquidity risk** — how it is managed, plus **maturity analyses** for portfolios of insurance contracts issued and reinsurance held that are liabilities, showing net cash flows for **each of the first five years** and in aggregate beyond, either as undiscounted contractual net cash flows or as present values (PAA/¶69–70A liabilities for remaining coverage may be excluded) (IFRS 17.132).

**Common pitfalls:**
- **Contract boundary set by cancellation rights rather than repricing rights.** ¶34 turns on the *practical ability to reassess risk and reprice to fully reflect it* — at individual or portfolio level — not on whether the policyholder can lapse. Portfolio-level repricing only closes the boundary if premiums up to the reassessment date also ignore post-reassessment risks.
- **Reassessing the level of aggregation after inception.** Groups are fixed at initial recognition and are never re-composed (IFRS 17.24) — even if a profitable group later becomes onerous.
- **Treating ¶20 (law or regulation constraining pricing) as a general grouping relief.** The standard expressly forbids applying it by analogy.
- **Using the EU annual-cohorts exemption while asserting full IFRS compliance.** An entity that uses it cannot make an explicit and unreserved statement of compliance with IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the IASB, and must disclose that it used the exemption. This is the single most common IFRS-vs-EU-IFRS reconciliation issue for European life insurers.
- **Failing the PAA eligibility test by treating "one year or less" as the only route, or by ignoring ¶54.** The one-year route is measured on the coverage period of **each contract including all premiums within the contract boundary** — multi-year renewable business often fails it. And the materiality route is disqualified outright by expected significant variability in the FCF affecting the LRC before a claim is incurred, which embedded derivatives and long coverage periods drive (IFRS 17.54).
- **Defaulting PAA revenue to passage of time without testing the risk-release pattern.** ¶B126 requires the expected-timing-of-incurred-expenses basis where the release of risk differs **significantly** from the passage of time — which is the norm for seasonal, catastrophe and construction-period covers — and ¶B127 requires the basis to change as facts change.
- **Assuming PAA eliminates the risk adjustment or discounting.** The LIC still requires an RA, and still requires discounting where claims are expected to settle more than a year after being incurred (IFRS 17.59(b)).
- **Accreting interest on the VFA CSM at a locked-in rate.** There is no interest-accretion step and no locked-in rate under ¶45 — the VFA CSM is remeasured through the entity's share of the underlying items. Conversely, applying **current** rates to GMM CSM adjustments is wrong: ¶B72(b)–(c) require the **initial-recognition** rates for both accretion and future-service FCF changes.
- **Adjusting the GMM CSM for experience variances or LIC remeasurements.** Only the ¶B96 items adjust the CSM; ¶B97 items — financial risk effects, LIC estimate changes and all other experience adjustments — go to P&L or OCI.
- **Allocating favourable future-service changes to the CSM while a loss component still exists.** ¶50(b) requires them to go **solely to the loss component until it is nil**, and only the excess reinstates the CSM.
- **VFA assessed on two conditions instead of three, or on a pool that is not clearly identified.** A crediting rate or dividend set at the end of the period, with no identified underlying items, fails ¶B106(b) even though it looks participating. And VFA classification is **locked at inception** (IFRS 17.B102) — it cannot be revisited when the economics change.
- **Applying VFA to reinsurance held.** Prohibited by ¶B109, regardless of the underlying contracts.
- **Claiming a reinsurance loss-recovery component for retrospectively purchased cover.** ¶B119C requires the reinsurance to have been entered into **before or at the same time as** the onerous underlying contracts are recognised.
- **Recognising a net gain on reinsurance held immediately.** It is deferred as the reinsurance CSM (IFRS 17.65); only a net cost relating to **past events** is expensed immediately (IFRS 17.65A).
- **Presenting reinsurance held as a reduction in revenue, or netting it against contracts issued.** ¶82 requires separate presentation; ¶86(c) prohibits presenting the allocation of premiums paid as a reduction in revenue; ¶78 requires four separate statement-of-financial-position captions.
- **Presenting premiums written as revenue.** ¶85 prohibits presenting premium information in P&L where it is inconsistent with the ¶83 services-provided basis, and requires investment components to be excluded from both revenue and expenses.
- **Assuming the OCI option is a single entity-wide election.** It is applied **per portfolio** (IFRS 17.B129), and the ¶88(b) and ¶89(b) variants recycle differently on derecognition (IFRS 17.91).
- **Using asset yields to build the discount rate.** ¶36 and ¶B79 require the **liquidity characteristics of the insurance contracts**; a top-down curve must strip out credit risk and other asset-specific factors (IFRS 17.B83).
- **Straight-lining the CSM.** ¶B119 requires coverage units reflecting the **quantity of benefits** and expected coverage period, extended for investment-return service only where all three ¶B119B conditions hold.
- **Treating modified retrospective as a free election.** It is available only where full retrospective is impracticable, each modification only to the extent the information for retrospective application is unavailable (IFRS 17.C8), and the entity must fall back to fair value where the MRA information is unavailable (IFRS 17.C6(a)).
- **Under-disclosing the transition split.** ¶114 requires the CSM reconciliation and insurance revenue to stay split between MRA, FVA and other contracts for as long as those groups exist — not just in the year of adoption.
- **Disclosing a risk adjustment technique without the equivalent confidence level.** ¶119 requires the confidence level corresponding to the result of whatever technique is used (e.g. cost of capital).
- **Assuming a non-insurer cannot have IFRS 17 contracts.** The March 2025 agenda decision is explicit that the scoping requirements govern, not the nature of the entity's business activities — a corporate guaranteeing a joint venture's obligations, or a bank writing a non-debt-instrument guarantee, can fall into IFRS 17. The prior "explicit assertion plus insurance accounting" condition in ¶7(e) is a **historical fact about the issuer**, not an election available now.
- **Forgetting IFRS 17 groups are monetary items in a foreign currency.** ¶30 requires the whole group, **including the CSM**, to be treated as monetary under IAS 21; the September 2022 IFRIC agenda decision prohibits presuming the CSM is denominated in the functional currency.

**IFRIC agenda decisions in force (IFRS 17):**
- **Transfer of Insurance Coverage under a Group of Annuity Contracts** (June 2022) — coverage units for immediate and deferred annuities; reproduced in the annotated standard at ¶B119.
- **IFRS 17 and IAS 21 — Multi-currency Groups of Insurance Contracts** (September 2022) — the entity must develop an accounting policy determining the currency denomination of the group (single- or multi-currency) and cannot presume the CSM is in the functional currency; the choice determines which FX effects are IFRS 17 financial risk and which are IAS 21 exchange differences; reproduced at ¶30.
- **Premiums Receivable from an Intermediary (IFRS 17 and IFRS 9)** (September 2023; IASB did not object, October 2023) — fact pattern: the policyholder has paid the premiums in cash to an intermediary but the insurer has not yet received them, and if the intermediary fails to pay, the insurer **cannot recover from the policyholder or cancel the contract**. ¶33 and ¶B65 bring premiums collected through an intermediary inside the contract boundary, and ¶B65 does not distinguish direct from intermediated collection. But IFRS 17 is **silent** on whether cash flows are removed from the measurement of a group only when recovered or settled in cash. The insurer therefore develops an IAS 8 accounting policy, choosing between:
  - **View 1 — remove on cash recovery.** Non-PAA: the premiums stay in the measurement of the group as future cash flows until recovered in cash. **PAA: the insurer does not increase the liability for remaining coverage until it recovers the cash from the intermediary.**
  - **View 2 — remove on discharge of the policyholder's obligation.** Non-PAA: remove the premiums from the group's measurement and recognise a **separate IFRS 9 financial asset**. PAA: increase the LRC **and** recognise a separate IFRS 9 financial asset.
  - The choice determines whether expected credit losses on the receivable are dealt with under IFRS 17 (with ¶131 credit-risk disclosure) or under IFRS 9 (with IFRS 7 disclosures) — and, under PAA, the timing of LRC recognition. Both views were accepted as providing useful information. Reproduced inline at ¶33 in the 2026 edition.
- **Guarantees Issued on Obligations of Other Entities** (March 2025) — **new since the 2025 edition of the standard; reproduced in IFRS 9 Appendix A and cross-referenced from IFRS 17 ¶3.** Sets the sequencing an issuer must follow for any guarantee it writes, and states expressly that the answer turns on the **scoping requirements in the Standards, not on the nature of the entity's business activities** — a bank or corporate can end up in IFRS 17 just as an insurer can. The entity must analyse **all** terms and conditions, explicit or implicit, unless they have no substance. Order of assessment:
  1. **Is it a financial guarantee contract?** If so, IFRS 9 (with IAS 32 and IFRS 7) applies — unless the issuer has previously **asserted explicitly** that it regards such contracts as insurance contracts and has used insurance accounting, in which case it may elect IFRS 9 or IFRS 17, contract by contract, irrevocably [IFRS 9 ¶2.1(e)(iii); IFRS 17 ¶7(e)]. The Committee acknowledged **diversity in practice in interpreting "debt instrument"** in the financial guarantee definition, concluded that judgement must be applied, and noted the IASB decided (April 2024) to consider the broader financial-guarantee questions at its **next agenda consultation** — so this is unresolved and live.
  2. **If not a financial guarantee contract, is it an insurance contract as defined in IFRS 17?** IFRS 17 applies to all insurance contracts regardless of the type of entity issuing them. Apply the ¶B2–B30 guidance on the definition and on significant insurance risk, then the scoping requirements in ¶3–13 — in particular the ¶7 exclusions and the ¶8 / ¶8A elections.
  3. **If neither**, consider IFRS 9 (loan commitment under ¶2.3, derivative, or otherwise a financial liability under IAS 32), IFRS 15 (where the counterparty is a customer and no other Standard applies), or IAS 37 (only where a provision, contingent liability or contingent asset arises outside other Standards).
  - The Committee concluded the existing principles are an adequate basis and added no project.

**Related standards:** IFRS 9 (assets backing insurance liabilities; separation of embedded derivatives ¶11(a); the transition classification overlay ¶C28A–C28E and redesignation ¶C29; financial guarantee contracts ¶7(e)), IFRS 13 (fair value of underlying items under VFA; fair value approach at transition ¶C20, which disapplies IFRS 13.47), IFRS 7 (financial risk disclosures for assets, and for premium receivables under View 2 of the 2023 agenda decision), IFRS 15 (distinct non-insurance goods and services ¶12; the fixed-fee service election ¶8; product warranties ¶7(a)), IFRS 16 (residual value guarantees and variable lease payments ¶7(c)–(d)), IFRS 18 (statement of profit or loss line items 75(c), operating classification of insurance finance income/expenses 64(b), gross-profit-like subtotals B123(c)–(d); amended IFRS 17 ¶91, 96, 103, B129), IFRS 19 (reduced disclosures for eligible subsidiaries; referenced at ¶C3(a)), IFRS 3 (contracts acquired in a business combination ¶39, ¶B93–B95F; contingent consideration excluded ¶7(f)), IAS 21 (insurance contract groups including the CSM are monetary items ¶30), IAS 32 (financial guarantee contracts under the ¶7(e) election), IAS 34 (interim reporting policy choice ¶B137), IAS 37 (excluded from IFRS 17 scope), IAS 19 / IAS 26 (employee benefit and retirement plan exclusions ¶7(b)), IFRS 4 (superseded from 1 January 2023).

---

## IFRS 18 — Presentation and Disclosure in Financial Statements

**Scope:** Applies to all general purpose financial statements prepared in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards. Sets requirements for the presentation of the primary financial statements and for disclosure in the notes. Supersedes IAS 1 in its entirety (IFRS 18.C8). Recognition and measurement are unaffected — IFRS 18 changes *where* and *how* amounts appear, not what those amounts are.

**Status and amendments:** Issued 9 April 2024; effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after 1 January 2027; earlier application permitted, and an early adopter must disclose that fact in the notes (IFRS 18.C1). EU-endorsed 13 February 2026, published in the Official Journal 16 February 2026, EU effective date 1 January 2027 (EFRAG Endorsement Status Report, 23 July 2026); **UK-adopted 10 December 2025** (UKEB, adoption statement published 12 December 2025). Unlike IFRS 19, IFRS 18 is **mandatory** — IFRS 18.C1 says an entity *shall* apply it. Appendix D makes consequential amendments to other Standards, applied for periods beginning on or after 1 January 2027 or earlier if IFRS 18 is applied early. Four IFRIC agenda decisions on IFRS 18 were published in April 2026 (see **IFRIC agenda decisions** below); the IASB has withdrawn three IAS 1-era agenda decisions with effect from 1 January 2027.

**Core principle:** Financial statements must communicate, not merely comply. The primary financial statements provide *useful structured summaries* of recognised assets, liabilities, equity, income, expenses and cash flows; the notes provide the material information needed to understand those summaries and to supplement them (IFRS 18.16–18). Three substantive changes drive everything else: a defined five-category structure with two mandated subtotals in the statement of profit or loss; mandatory disclosure of management-defined performance measures; and explicit principles governing aggregation and disaggregation.

**Key rules:**

- **Complete set of financial statements (IFRS 18.10):** statement(s) of financial performance; statement of financial position; statement of changes in equity; statement of cash flows; notes; comparative information (paras 31–32); and a third statement of financial position where para 37 requires one. The first four (with comparatives) are the **primary financial statements** (para 11). All primary statements are presented with equal prominence (para 14).
  - Statement(s) of financial performance may be a single combined statement (profit or loss section first, then OCI) or a separate statement of profit or loss immediately preceding a statement presenting comprehensive income (IFRS 18.12).
  - Alternative titles are permitted ('balance sheet', 'net income') provided the label faithfully represents the item (IFRS 18.11, .43).

- **The five categories of the statement of profit or loss (IFRS 18.47):** operating, investing, financing, income taxes, discontinued operations. Classification rules:
  - **Operating (para 52)** — the residual. All income and expenses *not* classified in the investing, financing, income taxes or discontinued operations categories. It is not defined by what it contains; it is defined by what it excludes.
  - **Investing (paras 53–54)** — income and expenses from (a) investments in associates, joint ventures and unconsolidated subsidiaries; (b) cash and cash equivalents; and (c) other assets that generate a return **individually and largely independently of the entity's other resources**. The income and expenses caught are the return generated, initial and subsequent measurement effects including derecognition, and incremental directly attributable acquisition/disposal costs (para 54).
    - Assets typically inside para 53(c): debt and equity investments; investment properties and receivables for rent from them (B46). Income captured: interest, dividends, rental income, depreciation, impairment and reversals, fair value gains and losses, derecognition/held-for-sale remeasurement (B47).
    - Assets typically **outside** para 53(c) — used in combination, so operating: PP&E; receivables for goods and services; loans to customers where financing customers is a main business activity (B48). Income from these is operating, including a bargain purchase gain and contingent consideration remeasurements where the acquired assets feed operating (B49(f)).
  - **Financing (paras 59–66)** — turns on a distinction the entity must draw first (para 59):
    - *Liabilities from transactions that involve **only** the raising of finance* (para 59(a); B50–B51): the entity receives finance (cash, extinguishment of a financial liability, or its own equity instruments) and will later return cash or own equity. Debentures, loans, notes, bonds, mortgages; **the liability under a supplier finance arrangement once the trade payable is derecognised**; a bond settled in own shares; an obligation to buy back own equity. **All** income and expenses from initial and subsequent measurement, derecognition, and incremental issue/extinguishment costs go to financing (para 60; B52 — interest expense, FVTPL fair value movements, dividends on shares classified as liabilities).
    - *Liabilities from transactions that do **not** involve only the raising of finance* (para 59(b); B53): trade payables, contract liabilities, lease liabilities, defined benefit obligations, decommissioning and restoration provisions, litigation provisions. Only two things go to financing (para 61): **interest income and expenses**, and **income and expenses arising from changes in interest rates** — and only if the entity already identifies those amounts to apply another IFRS requirement. So: IFRS 16 lease interest, IFRS 15 significant-financing-component interest, IAS 19 net interest, and IAS 37 unwinding of discount and discount-rate change effects are financing (B54); IAS 19 current and past service cost, and the expense for consuming the goods or services purchased on credit, stay operating (B55).
    - Excluded from financing and forced into operating (para 64): income and expenses from issued investment contracts with participation features under IFRS 9, and IFRS 17 insurance finance income and expenses.
  - **Income taxes (para 67)** — tax expense or income within the scope of IAS 12, plus related foreign exchange differences.
  - **Discontinued operations (para 68)** — as required by IFRS 5.
  - **B29 sets the order of operations:** items classified in the **discontinued operations** category under para 68 are not subject to the classification requirements for the categories in paras 47(a)–(d); items classified in the **income taxes** category under para 67 are not subject to those for paras 47(a)–(c). So the sequence is: discontinued operations first, then income taxes, then investing and financing, and operating takes what is left.

- **Foreign exchange differences (B65–B69):** classify in the **same category as the income and expenses from the item that gave rise to them**, unless doing so involves undue cost or effort — in which case operating (B65, B68). The undue cost or effort assessment is made **for each item** that gives rise to FX differences, not at portfolio level (B68). Where a single transaction that does not involve only raising finance produces income in more than one category, judgement determines which single category the FX difference follows — **allocation across categories is prohibited** (B67). Similar liabilities must be treated consistently. IAS 29 net monetary position gain or loss goes to operating unless presented with the associated income and expenses (B69).

- **Derivatives and hedging instruments (B70–B76):**
  - Designated hedging instruments: gains and losses follow the category of the income and expenses affected by the risk being hedged (B70). Undesignated components and ineffective portions follow the designated/effective portion (B71).
  - Undesignated derivatives **used to manage identified risks**: same rule (B72), but if it would require grossing up, **or** involve undue cost or effort, everything goes to operating.
  - Derivatives **not** used to manage identified risks (B73): financing if the derivative relates to a transaction involving only the raising of finance; otherwise operating.
  - **Grossing up** (B74–B75) arises only where the derivative manages the risks of a group of items with offsetting risk positions *and* those risks affect line items in more than one category — presenting the derivative gain or loss in each category would require showing a larger amount in each category than actually occurred. Prohibited. This is the mechanism, and the April 2026 agenda decision turns on it (below).

- **The two required subtotals (IFRS 18.69–72):**
  - **Operating profit or loss** — all income and expenses classified in the operating category (para 70).
  - **Profit or loss before financing and income taxes** — operating profit or loss plus all income and expenses classified in the investing category (para 71).
  - Profit or loss — the total of all five categories (para 72; carried forward from IAS 1).
  - **The one exception (para 73):** an entity that provides financing to customers as a main business activity **and** elects under para 65(a)(ii) to classify in operating the income and expenses from liabilities that do not relate to providing financing to customers **must not** present 'profit or loss before financing and income taxes'. It applies para 24 instead to decide whether some other subtotal is needed after operating profit. If it presents a subtotal comprising operating profit plus all investing-category items, it **must not** label it in a way implying financing is excluded — 'profit before financing' is specifically prohibited (para 74).

- **Entities with specified main business activities (paras 49–51; B30–B41)** — where most of the complexity lives:
  - There are exactly two specified main business activities: **investing in particular types of assets**, and **providing financing to customers** (para 49). An entity may have more than one main business activity (B30); it need only determine whether either or both of these two apply.
  - Effect (para 50): such an entity classifies **in operating** some income and expenses that would otherwise be investing or financing.
  - It is **a matter of fact, not assertion**, and must be based on evidence (B33). Indicators: use of a gross-profit-like subtotal (see B123) as an important indicator of operating performance, whether used externally or internally (B34–B35); an IFRS 8 **reportable** segment comprising a single business activity indicates a main business activity, and an **operating** segment comprising a single business activity indicates one *might* exist (B36).
  - Examples given: investing in assets — investment entities as defined in IFRS 10, investment property companies, insurers (B31). Providing financing to customers — banks and other lenders, entities financing customer purchases of the entity's own products, lessors providing finance leases (B32). These lists are **not exhaustive** (confirmed by the April 2026 agenda decision).
  - The assessment is made **for the reporting entity as a whole** (B37) — so a consolidated group and a subsidiary within it can legitimately reach different answers, and a parent's separate financial statements can differ from the group's.
  - Granularity of assessment: for associates/JVs/unconsolidated subsidiaries not equity-accounted, by individual asset or by groups sharing characteristics; in separate financial statements the groups must align with the IAS 27.10 measurement categories (B38). For para 53(c) assets, individually or by groups; for financial assets, groups consistent with the IFRS 7.6 classes (B40). No assessment is needed for equity-accounted investments (their income is always investing, para 55(a)) or for cash and cash equivalents (B39).
  - **Prospective only (B41):** the assessment is based on facts at the time. A change in outcome is applied prospectively from the date of change; prior amounts are **not** reclassified. Disclosure of the before/after amounts and classifications is required unless impracticable (para 51(c)(ii)).
  - **Investing in assets as a main business activity:**
    - Associates, JVs, unconsolidated subsidiaries (para 55): **investing** if equity-accounted; **operating** if not equity-accounted.
    - Other para 53(c) assets (para 58): operating.
    - Cash and cash equivalents (para 56): investing, unless (a) the entity invests as a main business activity in para 53(c) financial assets — then operating; or (b) it provides financing to customers as a main business activity (below).
  - **Providing financing to customers as a main business activity:**
    - Cash and cash equivalents (para 56(b)): the part relating to providing financing to customers (e.g. balances held for related regulatory requirements) → operating; the part not relating to it → **accounting policy choice**, operating or investing.
    - Liabilities that involve only raising finance (para 65(a)): those relating to providing financing to customers → operating; those not → **accounting policy choice**, operating or financing.
    - **The two policy choices must be made consistently with each other** (paras 56(b)(ii) and 65(a)(ii) each cross-refer to the other). If the entity cannot distinguish the two populations, it must apply the policy choice so that **all** of them go to operating (paras 57 and 66).
    - Liabilities that do not involve only raising finance (para 65(b)): para 61 items → financing; everything else → operating.

- **Presentation of expenses in the operating category (paras 78–85):**
  - Present operating expenses in a way that provides the **most useful structured summary**, using nature, function, or **both** (para 78). Any single line item must be aggregated on only one of the two bases, but different line items may use different bases (para 79).
  - Factors for choosing (B80): what best shows the main components or drivers of profitability; what most closely reflects how the business is managed and reported internally; standard industry practice; and whether allocation to functions would be arbitrary — if it would be, classify **by nature**.
  - Mixed presentation is expressly contemplated (B81). If used, labels must make clear what each line item contains — e.g. 'employee benefits other than those included in cost of sales' (B82).
  - Consistency period to period (B83); a nil amount in a later period is not a change in classification.
  - If any function line item is presented: a separate **cost of sales** line item is required where the entity uses a cost of sales function, and it must include the total inventory expense described in IAS 2.38; and a **qualitative description of the nature of expenses in each function line item** must be disclosed (para 82).
  - Level of aggregation is itself a judgement (B85) — selling and administrative expenses have dissimilar characteristics and may need separating.

- **Specified expenses by nature — the "by function trigger" (para 83):** an entity presenting **one or more line items comprising expenses classified by function in the operating category** must disclose **in a single note**:
  - (a) the total for each of five specified natures: **depreciation** (the amounts required by IAS 16.73(e)(vii), IAS 40.79(d)(iv) and IFRS 16.53(a)); **amortisation** (IAS 38.118(e)(vi)); **employee benefits** (the IAS 19 amount plus the IFRS 2 amount for services received from employees); **impairment losses and reversals** (IAS 36.126(a) and 126(b)); and **write-downs and reversals of write-downs of inventories** (IAS 2.36(e) and 36(f));
  - (b) for each of those five totals, the amount related to **each line item in the operating category**, and a list of any line items **outside** the operating category that also include amounts relating to the total.
  - The amounts disclosed **need not be the amounts recognised as expense in the period** — they can include amounts capitalised into the carrying amount of an asset. If so, a qualitative explanation identifying the assets involved is required (B84(b)). Where expenses are *presented* by nature and the amounts are not the expense recognised, an additional line item for the change in carrying amount of the affected assets is presented instead (B84(a); e.g. the IAS 2.39 changes-in-inventories line).
  - **Relief (paras 84–85):** an entity applying para 83 is exempt from the para 41 disaggregation requirement to the extent of further nature-within-function detail beyond the para 83 amounts, in both directions. The exemption does not override specific disclosure requirements about those expenses elsewhere in IFRS Accounting Standards.

- **Aggregation and disaggregation (paras 41–43; B78–B79):**
  - Classify and aggregate on **shared** characteristics; disaggregate on characteristics that are **not shared**; aggregate/disaggregate so that primary statements fulfil their role and the notes fulfil theirs; and ensure neither obscures material information (para 41).
  - **Disaggregate whenever the resulting information is material** (para 42). If material information is not presented in a primary statement, it must be disclosed in the notes.
  - Characteristics to test against (B78): nature; function; **persistence** (frequency, recurring vs non-recurring); measurement basis; measurement or outcome uncertainty; size; geography or regulatory environment; tax effects; and whether the amount arises on initial recognition or from a subsequent change in estimate.
  - Named candidates for separate presentation or disclosure (B79): inventory write-downs and reversals; PP&E impairments and reversals; restructuring income and expenses and reversals of restructuring provisions; gains and losses on disposals of PP&E; gains and losses on disposals of investments; litigation settlements; reversals of provisions; other non-recurring items.
  - Labels must faithfully represent the item, including the meaning of terms used and how amounts were aggregated (para 43).

- **The "useful structured summary" mechanism (paras 21–24):** the structure of each primary statement is fixed by named paragraphs — profit or loss by paras 47, 69, 76 and 78; comprehensive income by paras 86–88; financial position by paras 96 and 104; changes in equity by para 107; cash flows by IAS 7.10 (para 22). Within that structure:
  - A specifically required line item **need not be presented separately** if doing so is not necessary for a useful structured summary (para 23) — even where a Standard describes the list as minimum requirements.
  - Additional line items and subtotals **must** be presented where necessary for a useful structured summary (para 24), and must comprise IFRS-measured amounts, be compatible with the mandated structure, be consistent period to period, and be **no more prominent** than IFRS-required totals and subtotals.

- **Statement of financial position (paras 96–106):** current/non-current classification unless a liquidity presentation provides a more useful structured summary (para 96); amounts recoverable/settled after more than 12 months disclosed for mixed line items (para 97); deferred tax never current (para 98).
  - Current assets (para 99): realised/sold/consumed in the normal operating cycle; held primarily for trading; realised within 12 months; or cash/cash equivalents unless restricted from being exchanged or used to settle a liability for at least 12 months.
  - Current liabilities (para 101): settled in the normal operating cycle; held primarily for trading; due within 12 months; or **no right at the end of the reporting period to defer settlement for at least 12 months**.
  - The covenants rules carried forward from the 2020/2022 IAS 1 amendments (B99–B106): the right must have **substance** and exist at period end (B99); covenants the entity must comply with **on or before** period end affect whether the right exists — even if compliance is only tested afterwards; covenants that only bite **after** period end do not (B100). Breach on or before period end with the liability becoming payable on demand → current, even if the lender agrees not to demand payment before the accounts are authorised (B102); a period of grace agreed **by** period end and running at least 12 months → non-current (B103). Classification is unaffected by the likelihood of exercising the right, or by actual settlement before authorisation (B104). Post-period-end refinancing, rectification and grants of grace are **non-adjusting** IAS 10 events (B105).
  - **Covenant disclosure (B106):** where liabilities are non-current because the covenants bite only after period end, disclose the nature of the covenants and when compliance is required, the carrying amount of the related liabilities, and any facts and circumstances indicating possible difficulty complying — expressly including that the entity would **not** have complied had the covenants been tested on period-end circumstances.
  - Required line items (para 103): PP&E; investment property; intangibles; goodwill; financial assets (excl. (g), (j), (k)); IFRS 17 portfolios that are assets; equity-accounted investments; biological assets; inventories; trade and other receivables; cash and cash equivalents; total assets held for sale and in disposal groups; trade and other payables; provisions; other financial liabilities; IFRS 17 portfolios that are liabilities; current tax assets and liabilities; deferred tax assets and liabilities; liabilities in disposal groups held for sale. Plus NCI and issued capital and reserves attributable to owners of the parent (para 104). Order and format are not prescribed (para 106).

- **Statement presenting comprehensive income (paras 86–95):** OCI in two categories — will be reclassified to profit or loss when conditions are met, and will not (para 88); within each, separate line items for the share of OCI of equity-accounted investees and for other OCI items (para 89). Reclassification adjustments presented or disclosed (para 90); tax on each OCI item presented or disclosed (para 93); OCI may be shown net of tax or gross with a single aggregate tax amount, in which case the tax must be split between the two categories (paras 94–95).

- **Notes (paras 113–116):** basis of preparation and material accounting policies are now signposted to **IAS 8 paras 6A–6N and 27A–27I** (para 113(a)). Notes presented in a systematic manner; each item in the primary statements cross-referenced to related note information; and **where amounts disclosed in a note are included in one or more primary-statement line items, the note must say which line items** (para 114) — a genuinely new requirement relative to IAS 1.113. Entity identification information in para 116.

**Management-defined performance measures (MPMs):**

- **Definition (para 117):** a **subtotal of income and expenses** that (a) the entity uses in **public communications outside** the financial statements; (b) is used to communicate management's view of an aspect of the financial performance of **the entity as a whole**; and (c) is **not** listed in para 118 and not a total or subtotal specifically required to be presented or disclosed by IFRS Accounting Standards.
- **The para 118 safe list — these are never MPMs:** gross profit or loss and similar subtotals (see B123); operating profit or loss before depreciation, amortisation and IAS 36 impairments; operating profit or loss plus income and expenses from all equity-accounted investments; for a para 73 entity, operating profit or loss plus all investing-category items; profit or loss before income taxes; profit or loss from continuing operations.
  - 'Similar to gross profit' means the difference between a type of revenue and the directly related expenses incurred in generating it (B123) — net interest income, net fee and commission income, insurance service result, net financial result, net rental income.
